data-go=\"253\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x053c02","title":"The Crocodile at the Bend","volume":"伙伴外传：The Bonneted Crocodile","anchor":"chapter-0254","paragraphs":["On the water, Rhea's crocodile moved with surprising grace. Idris's shoes remained dry, and his misgivings began to ebb. He examined the steady pump, then allowed himself to notice the flowers. Their blossoming paper colors made an aging skiff into something extravagant. Rhea stood at the tiller, intent and smiling. He had repaired enough engines to recognize a dangerous distraction. This one was every bit as attractive as it was inconvenient. The pump could eject water without carrying petals into the canal.","The parade announcer delivered a grand account of civilisation. A dragon of globalisation followed the polar bear of global warming, while a school float depicted the growth of a seed. Devi, seated safely on the bank with her friends, shouted that the children had achieved a better synthesis than the adults. Idris hoped she meant the artwork. Her smile suggested that she had more than one subject in mind.","Rhea had learned to live by weather and repair bills after the shipyard slump. The company's deregulatory rhetoric had promised unstoppable expansion; when orders fell, workers got laid off. She did not want Idris to mourn her old managerial title. She wanted him to pass the pole. His willing response made her relaxation more complete than any sympathetic speech could have done. Rhea would not dump the old skill with the lost job.","They reached the first turn behind the corporate swan. Hot air from its fan began to frizzle a hanging ribbon, curling its edge. Rhea called to its crew. A visible flame did not appear, but the smell was warning enough. The coordinator had them stop the fan and remove the loose decoration. A blaze of pride crossed the former boss's face when the crowd laughed, then dissipated as he noticed the scorched edge.","Idris would not criticize a person for stopping machinery. \"Good decision,\" he called to the crew.","The fan's guard was identifiable by a red stripe. Rhea did not deem the smoke irrelevant, whatever the owner said. In contrast to his inconsistent haste, Idris waited for the rotating parts to stop. Mindlessly repeating a reassuring label would not help if plastic began to melt. He could relate to the worker's embarrassment without letting the risk take hold.","Rhea looked at him. \"That ought to be ordinary.\"","\"It can become ordinary.\"","They could not halve the swan's weight or drastically shorten it. They could expedite its turn with two coordinated poles from the bank. The solution was not far-reaching, but it was adequate. A towline would go further than the former boss's claim that spectators should disperse. Rhea accepted the coordinator's request to wait while the larger boat moved.","Her mother sent a message: BEEN THROUGH CHILDBIRTH. THIS IS SLOWER. Rhea showed Idris, laughing. \"She assisted births. She also had me, obviously, which I hear was inconvenient.\"","\"The evidence is before us.\"","She hit him lightly with a paper flower. His smile was a rare bloom, brief enough to make her want another one.","A laminated map showed the next bend. The native-born rowers treated its changes to the old course with suspicion. Historically, their fathers had passed the willow, but erosion had weakened that bank. The governmental inspector's notice was clear. Rhea refused a unilateral shortcut even when the swan's owner proposed it. A permit could be toothless if everyone ignored it, and she had no wish to make hers meaningless.","The coincident delay gave them time to check the extraction hose. Idris noticed a tiny leak where it met the pump. The first test had been reproducible in calm water, but movement exposed a problem. He conceded the limit without embarrassment. Rhea's concurring nod was immediate; feedback should lead to a repair, not an argument. They used a spare fitting and checked it again.","The budding tree on the crocodile had lost a leaf. 'Beware of spring weather,' Rhea joked. 'My masterpiece contains a bit of everything.' Idris would not flatter her workmanship by pretending it was immortal. He would help ensure it did not deteriorate. In terms of service life, the boat still had years to offer, and his growing interest did not claim to be entirely professional.","An inefficient knot held the crocodile's jaw. Rhea's seemingly orthodox fix was to cut it. Idris was apprehensive until he realized the jaw was only decoration. It sprang open, revealing its toothless grin. A child screamed with delight. Their former boss's reaction was less cheerful. His extravagant swan could no longer compete with a bonneted reptile attempting to sing.","\"Don't perpetuate the story that technical people lack imagination,\" Rhea said.","\"I'm trying not to perpetuate a flood.\"","\"Intellectualism again.\"","He laughed. The banter was contagious. They could entertain each other without forgetting the route. His uncertainty diminished as he focused on a boat that answered her hand and a woman who did not mind his questions. The synthesis of his caution and her daring began to feel useful.","At the narrowest bend, a petal caught in the pump guard. Rhea reached for it, then withdrew before touching a moving part. They stopped the machine first. The small soak had made the paper weak enough to remove cleanly. Its absorption of water was the reason, Idris explained, then stopped as she grinned at him. \"You can tell me things,\" she said. \"I like how you think.\"","His answer was an unintelligible sound. The recorder on the bank might have caught it. He hoped not.","They restarted, leaving no scraps in the canal. The rules said deliberate dumping could be prosecuted, which Devi considered an overstatement until the coordinator explained the actual municipal prohibition on waste. A third party would collect any stranded flowers after the parade. The sponsor would not be solely responsible for choosing what counted as cleanup.","The final stretch demanded a slow turn toward the landing. Inflation, profit, old work, and cyclical disappointments fell away as Rhea looked at the finish. Her shop's future remained uncertain, yet the stagnant despair of the morning had lifted. She did not need Idris to promise that one prize would cure it. She needed a steady hand on the pole.","He gave her that. She brought the crocodile home, bonnet trembling above its outrageous smile."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1039,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0254\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Bonneted Crocodile</div><h1>The Crocodile at the Bend</h1><p id=\"chapter-0254-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5700\" data-words=\"5700\">On</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9133\" data-words=\"9133\">water</a>, Rhea&#x27;s crocodile <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5369\" data-words=\"5369\">moved</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9260\" data-words=\"9260\">with</a> surprising <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3667\" data-words=\"3667\">grace</a>. Pump","volume":"伙伴外传：The Bonneted Crocodile","anchor":"chapter-0255","paragraphs":["The judges awarded the bonneted crocodile first prize for bringing the river's growth to life. Rhea stared at the ribbon as if the lettering might change. Idris had expected to be relieved when he reached the bank. He was, but he was also sorry the small shared journey had ended. The stopped pump would no longer eject anything.","Devi arrived with a basket of food and a speech. Rhea cut the speech in half before it began. \"You assisted childbirth. You do not need to describe my entrance into civilisation to a man I've just met.\"","\"He ought to know what he's taking on.\"","\"He repaired a pump.\"","\"A blossoming friendship, then.\"","Her mother was unstoppable. Idris discovered that he did not mind. The aging midwife had the rare ability to flatter someone without making him feel exhibited. She thanked him for helping Rhea keep her ridiculous craft intact. Then she handed the ribbon to the child whose paper lily had survived its brief soak. The girl's smile was every bit as bright as the prize. The schoolchildren had explained climate change without pretending one float could solve it.","The polar bear of global warming won the school award. The dragon of globalisation won the largest entry category, narrowly defeating the swan. Its owner accepted the result without a managerial speech. Devi called that a synthesis of defeat and progress. Rhea begged her not to criticize him within hearing distance. A celebration need not perpetuate the morning's quarrel.","The river's cyclical ebb had begun. Crews would have to move carefully before the landing grew too shallow. Idris checked the coordinated retrieval plan and helped Rhea secure the extraction hose. A dump of leftover paper would undo the day's care, so they packed every flower for reuse. The seemingly fragile decorations were more identifiable after their minor repairs. Each now carried a little piece of the journey.","The private sector could find another repairer for its promotion. Rhea had no wish to mourn a title that had made her miserable. Inflation had shrunk the prize, but it would still drastically improve this month's breathing room. Her mother's story about the late 1970s was inaccurate by several years; correcting it would not entertain anyone. She let the cheerful exaggeration stand.","An organizer asked Rhea to record feedback. She described an inefficient waiting point and praised the clearer new route. The coincident fan problem on the swan had delayed them, but its crew's decision to stop was right. Her concurring view mattered to the worker standing nearby. He had expected the whole crowd to call him foolish. She would not diminish a sensible choice just because its owner had annoyed her.","The former boss approached later, his linen now damp. He offered Rhea a short repair contract, conditional on her publicly praising the company's deregulatory expansion. She declined without a speech. She would not live by a statement she did not believe. Nor could he deem a refusal evidence of ingratitude. Idris kept quiet, admiring her far more than a tactful admirer ought to reveal.","After the slump, she had feared she might get laid off from every job she accepted. The old insecurity could take hold quickly. Today's prize did not make her immune, yet the money would halve her rent bill for a month. Its effect was practical rather than far-reaching. She had no wish to exaggerate it. Idris liked her more for the clear arithmetic.","Devi led the children away before he could retreat into his tools. Their laughter dissipated down the bank. Suddenly he and Rhea had a quiet patch of grass, a warm meal, and no urgent problem between them. Relaxation was harder when he could no longer hide inside a task.","\"Do you always need a repair to talk to someone?\" she asked.","\"Only when I like them.\"","Idris was apprehensive when the crowd began to disperse. His own changing plans had an evolution he could scarcely explain. The growing affection might lead to dinner, and perhaps go further. He would not claim to predict it. In terms of romance, his experience was limited to watching other people succeed. Rhea let butter melt on a bun and waited for him.","Her grin softened. \"A promising defect.\"","He looked at his hands, then back at her. It was an orthodox beginning to a conversation he had no experience conducting. Intellectualism would not help. Neither would mindlessly pretending her meaning was irrelevant. He could be brave in an ordinary way without a storm or a broken engine to justify him.","\"Would you like dinner?\" he asked.","\"The buns are dinner.\"","\"Tomorrow.\"","She considered him long enough to make the canal seem stagnant. Then she said yes. His relief was so visible that she laughed, and the warmth became contagious again. He could relate to a repair manual much more easily than this new feeling, but he wanted to learn.","The race coordinator stopped by with the recovered scraps. No one would be prosecuted for an accidental lost petal; the waste rule concerned deliberate disposal. Rhea accepted the clarification and took the bag. Idris was glad the official distinction had been made quietly, without turning a pleasant day into another public argument.","The parked swan gave a final coughing burst as its crew tested the fan after removing the damaged ribbon. Nothing frizzled. The worker shut it off, and Rhea applauded. Its owner bowed with unexpected good humor. The little scene might expedite another conversation on another day. For now, Rhea wanted the river, not her former employer.","Idris helped her pull the skiff up. Historically, he would have invented another practical errand before leaving. In contrast, he now stood directly in front of her and asked when to arrive. \"Seven,\" she said. \"No pump.\"","\"No pump.\"","Devi's approval could be heard from the far side of the garden. Devi was proud of being native-born, but her daughter suspected that even a visitor could have found that convenient gap in the hedge. Her instinct for eavesdropping was apparently undiminished. Rhea shouted that she could stop listening. Her mother replied that she had never started.","Idris laughed, and Rhea took his hand. The boat rocked gently against its support. Its bonneted, toothless face looked indecently pleased."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1044,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0255\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Bonneted Crocodile</div><h1>Seven, without a Pump</h1><p id=\"chapter-0255-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4610\" data-words=\"4610\">judges</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-698\" data-words=\"698\">awarded</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> bonneted crocodile <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3261\" data-words=\"3261\">first</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6470\" data-words=\"6470\">prize</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3344\" data-words=\"3344\">for</a> bringing <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> river&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3743\" data-words=\"3743\">growth</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8538\" data-words=\"8538\">to</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4841\" data-words=\"4841\">life</a>. data-go=\"255\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x054c01","title":"Tall Man and the Tiny Wheel","volume":"伙伴外传：The Cart They Built","anchor":"chapter-0256","paragraphs":["Rowan Ash had once negotiated a loan worth a billion dollars. At the children's engineering fair, a girl of eight defeated him over a penny. \"You can't buy a better wheel,\" she said. \"You have to build one.\" A million coins could not purchase this particular exemption.","His toothy smile failed. \"An irrational restriction.\"","\"A rule.\"","Noor, his partner for the day, looked delighted. The fair's prospective inventors had been given a maximum budget and a minimum kit. Rowan's leadership skill would have to incorporate listening. Their team included a shy boy named Milo, a graceful girl named Saff, and a toy cart whose zip was currently theoretical.","The challenge was to move a small load across a kilometre of closed park paths. Every team started with one dollar, one euro, a penny, a shilling, and a cent. These were collectible tokens, not an exchange-rate test. A sign asked the children to choose which coin was oldest, then trade its picture for a clue. Rowan's knowledge of money was only marginal help. He could not live off his family name at a table where everyone called him \"Tall Man.\"","The kit instructions gave measurements in both systems. The platform was one foot long; its width appeared in centimetres and inches. A little gauge measured a millimeter gap at the axle. A conversion card used the words gram and gramme to show two spellings of the same unit. Noor asked Milo to explain which measurement related to the loose wheel. He pointed to the gap, then showed her the wobble.","A couple of volunteers tried to fix the video feed. Its bandwidth was inadequate for sixty simultaneous uploads. A judge who was eighty suggested waiting, and a man of ninety agreed. Their combined patience was formidable. The oldest rule sheet was out of date, so a volunteer replaced it. Rowan decided not to intellectualize the difficulty. He could move a dozen chairs while somebody solved the network.","\"A rational diagnosis,\" Rowan said.","\"It isn't a patient,\" Noor told him. \"Let him be an engineer.\"","The boy's grin made Rowan's correction painless. His good-natured surrender became more useful than his impatience. He staked out a patch of table for the parts while Saff checked the skeleton of the frame. They would take on one problem at a time, irrespective of the celebrity team assembling a prize-winning miniature racer beside them.","The adults had been banned from driving. Their job was to help children weigh options without intellectualizing every impulse. A prospective design could be tested with a minimal load first. The quota was sixteen wooden blocks, each marked with a letter. Their gross weight appeared on a sealed label, but the cart itself had to be weighed separately. The girl could distinguish gross from useful load without a speech about accounting.","The test scales displayed a decimal point in an odd place. A kilogram of metal apparently weighed a tenth of a gram. Saff stared. \"That's wrong.\"","\"Very wrong,\" Noor agreed.","The rechargeable scale had insufficient power. A volunteer replaced it with a reputable spring balance. Rowan set a solidly fixed bracket on the table and let the boy continue. A multitude of tiny washers had begun to perplex him, but the child seemed excited by their possibilities. The cart's cheerful disorder was unique enough to keep him interested.","A larger demonstration machine advertised fifty horsepower, a maximum speed far above the fair's limit, and a battery rating in volts. No child would operate it. Its motor's watt rating was explained on a separate board. Rowan read the labels with comparative humility. An old tractor pulled a ton of sand nearby, while their cart struggled with a kilo of blocks. The majority of progress, he suspected, consisted of accepting the scale of one's problem.","Noor handed out water. The bottles held a litre, and the café offered a pint or a quart of juice in reusable jugs. A gallon container supplied the wash station. A handwritten price card mixed ounces and grammes so bewilderingly that the vendor had attached a clearer one. He was approachable enough to laugh about it. The recipe had come from a grandfather who measured by handfuls.","A ruler a metre long, divided into centimetres, stood beside another marked by the inch. The vendor's own ninety-year-old mother had drawn the new card after seventy complaints, he joked. Each digit now made sense. The change prevented an irrational argument over the pound weight of fruit. Rowan began to seethe at a stuck clasp, then asked Saff for help. Her devastating smile suggested she had expected it.","\"A dosage of disorder,\" Noor said, examining the signs.","\"I can supply a stronger one,\" Rowan replied.","She gave him a look that frequently ruined his intended performance. Lately, he had begun to value it. He did not want to be a handsome distraction from work she enjoyed. Today, he would help the team rather than charm his way out of learning. He intended to abide by that promise, preferably before she teased him into keeping it.","A swath of bright tape marked the start. Teams would complete a quarter mile at a time, with a break after the second stage. The peak of the course was a gentle slope behind the bandstand. Its acre of grass would hold the finish celebration. The early forecast promised zero rain. Rowan distrusted any promise that simple.","The rival team leader arrived, smiling about their out-of-date cart. His children had a motor they could not use under the rules, and he was seething already. Milo watched him, suddenly mournful. Rowan wanted applause. Saff wanted the right to subtract it from the remaining distance without him explaining decimals. Noor took a photograph of his toothy attempt to look useful. \"Your leadership skill is developing,\" she said. Rowan began to seethe at the delay, then remembered whom the race belonged to.","\"A billion small humiliations.\"","\"Minimal discomfort. Excellent results.\"","The token quiz waited beside the bandstand. The children had to identify a penny, shilling, cent, dollar, and euro from their own kit. The prospective judge was a retired coin collector who refused to give adults hints. Milo answered four correctly, then asked a question about the fifth. His willingness to ask mattered more than immediate perfection. They earned their stamp together.","Milo wanted to incorporate a lighter handle. The quota was still sixteen blocks; making the skeleton lighter would help. Rowan could conceive a more elaborate system, but the boy's rational solution was better. The child, previously mournful, looked so pleased that Noor wanted to hug him. Instead, she asked whether he wanted a photograph of the new arrangement. Lately, she had become better at asking before assuming.","A swath of cloth caught in a wheel. Rowan stopped the cart and removed it after the children stepped clear. Its zip had become a snag. The related loss of time was marginal. Irrespective of the rival team's hurry, they would not take on the next slope with the axle jammed. Noor asked the boy to inspect it, and he found the remaining thread. He could not live off applause when his daughter wanted company.","The maximum incline had been tested with the same load. The sealed blocks weighed a kilo, with one marked \"gram\" and its partner \"gramme\" for the language game. Their gross weight included the bag. The children would not have to know a ton from a tonne to push a cart, but they would have to notice a dragging wheel. A minimal intervention made it roll again.","The rival father passed them, carrying his team's cart himself. His children trailed behind. He had entered a million-dollar idea competition once, he told Rowan, and would not lose to a toy. The word \"ourselves\" seemed to escape his vocabulary. His daughter asked for the handle. He let her take it, then kept directing every step until she stopped.","\"You can be first without me,\" she said.","The reply perplexed him. Noor watched the pair without supplying a verdict. They could have a conversation without an audience of helpful strangers. Rowan was learning the same restraint. A good-natured attempt to solve every person's problem could still make him the center of the day.","The second checkpoint asked them to compare a litre of water with a pint, quart, and gallon chart. Another card used ounce, pound, and gram. Saff liked the pattern. Milo liked the drawing of an absurdly large jug. Rowan liked the cake stand within sight. The fair's learning might have hidden inside pleasure without his needing to intellectualize it.","At the hill, a volunteer demonstrated a small generator. A label gave output in watt and volt units. Horsepower appeared on the old tractor's sign across the field. The units were not interchangeable, and the children did not pretend they were. They simply found the number the clue asked for, then checked its decimal point. Rowan wished his earliest finance teachers had made him do something equally concrete.","The next stage contained a harmless obstacle course: fifty flags, seventy colored cones, and a straight section measured in centimetres and millimeters. No one counted all of them during the race. The figures belonged to a scavenger card, giving waiting teams a way to play. A child called out eighty, corrected himself to ninety, and laughed. The minimum score was one completed question, not an exam in speed.","A boy with a chronic illness had a rest station prepared beside the course. His family had helped the organizers plan it. The child waved them on and promised to rejoin the last stretch if he felt able. Noor asked whether the water jug was still full, then left his care with the people who knew him. His presence needed no inspirational speech.","A couple of children had marked the final acre with a unique chalk design. It was bewilderingly elaborate and solidly placed on the dry path. The prize-winning demonstration cart was kept away from it. Its reputable owner proved approachable when asked to move, and the small courtesy prevented a potential collision. Rowan admired the graceful way he made room without becoming important about it.","Rowan's cart began to drift. The bracket's wear-and-tear had exposed a small bend. A split-second catch kept its load from tipping, and he stopped to examine it. Milo proposed moving the bag toward the middle. Noor asked how they could test the idea. They shifted only a handful of blocks first, and the frame sat more evenly. The full adjustment worked.","The girl called it their prime discovery. Rowan gave her a bow. The gesture excited a nearby toddler, who demanded one too. Rowan obliged so gracefully that the toddler clapped. His reputation with adults had been complicated; with very small children, he was apparently dependable.","At the peak, Saff wanted to celebrate. Rowan reminded her that the cart had to come down too. His caution did not disrupt her delight. She took the handle with Milo and moved at a comfortable pace. The adults followed. The rival father watched from a bench, his own team reassembling after a discussion that had clearly not been easy.","A mile marker stood near the exit from the larger park trail. Their own course was shorter, and the leaders had no reason to follow it. The boy checked the route anyway. It was a solid little decision that pleased Noor more than a flamboyant sprint. The majority of teams would finish within a few minutes of each other; the difference would not define the children.","Rowan had wanted a photograph with the prize. Now he wanted one with the people who had made the cart. He staked out a dry patch beyond the finish, promised everyone a drink, and let the last stage belong to the children. The zip of their wheels carried them unstoppably down the path."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1062,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0257\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Cart They Built</div><h1>A Hill with a Decimal Point</h1><p id=\"chapter-0257-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-593\" data-words=\"593\">At</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3261\" data-words=\"3261\">first</a> checkpoint, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1280\" data-words=\"1280\">cart</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> traveled <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2963\" data-words=\"2963\">exactly</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8353\" data-words=\"8353\">tenth</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4659\" data-words=\"4659\">kilometre</a>. →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x054c03","title":"A Kit Worth One Dollar","volume":"伙伴外传：The Cart They Built","anchor":"chapter-0258","paragraphs":["The cart reached the finish second, rolling over the line with a wooden block wobbling in its bag. Saff shouted. Milo laughed so hard he forgot to stop steering. Rowan caught the handle at the marked barrier, then joined their enormous celebration. Its maximum volume exceeded any reasonable expectation. Sixty guests cheered. The last inch had seemed irrationally long, Milo said; Noor agreed that tiredness could make a distance feel irrational.","\"A billion congratulations,\" he told the children.","\"That's too many to count,\" Milo said.","\"Then we'll round down.\"","The gross weight of the return kit appeared on its label. Saff had written it herself, checking the figure before giving it to Milo. A minimum of fuss, a multitude of thanks: that was her preferred arrangement. Rowan would not intellectualize such an excellent result. He had spent half the afternoon discovering that people could live off sandwiches and still have serious ambitions.","The prize was a small kit worth less than one dollar. The children would not have traded it for a euro, a shilling, a penny, or a cent from the quiz. It came with the right to exhibit their cart until the end of the day. Saff placed the language blocks on top, one gram beside one gramme, and informed everyone that her machine possessed international sophistication.","Noor had been given a special ribbon for patience. Rowan tried to claim that his leadership skill deserved one too. She asked which moment he meant. He wisely referred to Milo's suggestion about moving the load. The boy blushed, then explained it again to a visitor. A marginal adjustment had produced a meaningful change, and he knew why.","The rival father came over with his daughter. He apologized for his earlier remarks, without asking his children to endorse the apology. They had rejoined the course after deciding to finish under their own po
